# GENESIS-TEST.md — the OTV4TEST revision ledger

**This is the fork's ledger. `docs/GENESIS.md` beside it is FROZEN.**

`OTV4TEST` was populated on 2026-09-08 by pushing a full clone of
`TX-9AI/options_trader_v4` — 3,050 objects, complete history — to a new private
repo. Every revision in the inherited `docs/GENESIS.md` describes work that
landed on the **mainline fleet**, and it is kept unedited as the lineage record
of exactly where this fork came from. Nothing appends to it here.

Revisions made **in this repo** are numbered from **r1** and land here.

⚠️ **THE NUMBERING RESTARTS AND THAT IS DELIBERATE.** A fork revision is not a
fleet revision, and continuing mainline's sequence would make `r323` ambiguous
across two repos and two behaviours. When a rewire is proven and merges back,
the mainline revision that carries it gets its own mainline number and cites
the OTV4TEST revision by name — `OTV4TEST r7`, never a bare `r7`. That is the
same cross-repo citation discipline that produced a checker defect on mainline
(DOC.17, 2026-09-08: `check_ledger_parity` read `dtp rNNN` as its own numbers
and was quiet on 28 of 31 collisions purely by luck). Prefix the repo. Always.

⚠️ **APPEND-ONLY, AND THE LANDER DOES THE APPENDING.** `tools/land.sh` writes
one row per revision from the half's `DESC`, before `git add`, so the row ships
inside the commit it describes. Do not hand-edit a row to fix a mistake —
supersede it with a later row. The one exception mainline arrived at, after the
operator's ruling on 2026-09-08, is a **correction to the ledger itself**, which
may ship in an archive because `BASE` refuses a stale clone.

⚠️ **WHAT A FORK REVISION IS FOR.** This repo exists to prove PLAN_SPEC §10 —
the plan searches and selects, the strategy declares bars and confirms. A row
here should say what was rewired, what the plan now selects that the strategy
used to, and what evidence says it can fire. See `docs/FORK_BRIEF.md`.

| rev | what landed |
|---|---|

OTV4TEST was populated on 2026-09-08 by pushing a full clone of TX-9AI/options_trader_v4 (3050 objects, complete history) to a new private repo, with a repo-scoped deploy key so the box can push here and reach nothing else. This revision makes the fork self-sufficient off control. tools/land.sh v1.11 gains LEDGER in the land.spec, naming the ledger a half appends to - absent means docs/GENESIS.md so every mainline half is byte-for-byte unaffected, and this repo passes docs/GENESIS-TEST.md. A MISSING LEDGER NOW REFUSES rather than skipping the append - until now the append ran only if the file existed, so a typo would have committed with NO ledger row and reported success, violating section 35 silently inside the tool that enforces it. tools/check_land_discipline.py v1.2 gains --ledger to match, defaulting to GENESIS.md. docs/GENESIS-TEST.md is the fork ledger, numbered from r1; the inherited docs/GENESIS.md is FROZEN as the lineage record of where this fork came from and nothing appends to it here. THE NUMBERING RESTARTS DELIBERATELY - a fork revision is not a fleet revision, and a merge back cites OTV4TEST rN by name and never a bare rN, which is the exact cross-repo discipline whose absence produced DOC.17 on mainline where a checker read the other repo numbers as its own and was quiet on 28 of 31 collisions purely by luck. devtools.sh v4.3 gains item 40, LAND a tarball from home, which runs the ARCHIVE'S land.sh rather than the repo's copy so a delivery that fixes the lander is landed by the lander it ships and a fresh box bootstraps by the same path it uses forever after. docs/FORK_BRIEF.md is the charter for the rewire - the measured current boundary where the strategy selects and the plan merely evaluates, the PLAN_SPEC section 10 target where the plan searches and selects and the strategy declares bars and confirms, the ledger vocabulary of none available, decline with the bar named and the gap measured, and setup selected, the isolation configuration, and the acceptance test which is CAN IT FIRE and never P and L. | **r10** | r10 - THE CONDOR MANAGEMENT PLAN. Operator 2026-09-09: permissive, opportunistic and resilient - with a final trick if the mundane defense (roll for a free leg) is not possible: go inverted and buy a hedge leg on the other side. It is a MANAGEMENT PLAN, not a strategy - a condor is never entered, it FORMS when a second credit vertical pairs with the first, sweep-sweep or TCS-sweep, the complement always a sweep (PLAN_SPEC section 35); the fork-anchored condor entry is retired as a strategy (main v4.44 says so on its row instead of the dispatch-gap NOT ASKED) and the 1h fork stays a legitimate level through the tines in the store. THE ROW NAMES ITS WAIT: a lone credit vertical's CondorManagement row carries the complement it is waiting on, read from the sweep plan's last preparation on the authorized side (sweep_plan v1.1 LAST_PREP) - lone call vertical, waiting on a sweep REJECTED below, london 713.50 would sell 713/712P for 0.19 R 0.23. Found and fixed on the way: the management plan only recognised legs already flagged is_condor_leg, so a lone sweep or TCS vertical read as no credit verticals open (iron_condor_strategy v4.10 - defect 11 in the manifest). TESTED IS A WICK, NOT A DISTANCE (condor_roll v4.7): with the 1m frame, a side is tested when the closed bar's wick reached its short strike with the close still inside, breached on a close beyond (the tent's business, unchanged), and the old one-strike proximity rule survives only without a tape; main hands the roll the frame. The three rungs map to the code's ladder exactly - roll to risk-free, invert (the untested side rolled adjacent to the tested short), the tent (take the profitable vertical off, buy an opposite-type long equidistant from the remaining short; price under the tent) - and the row now says what the tent does honestly: it removes REVERSAL risk and turns a reversal into unbounded upside while the tested side's loss stays capped at its wing. THE FINAL-FORM FLOOR IS FROM WHERE IT STANDS (operator): after the tent the only exit is a 15 percent loss from the structure AS FORMED - the cost to close at formation, kept vertical's mark less the hedge's, grown 15 percent - NOT 15 percent of cumulative credit; cumulative credit stays on the record as the account of what was collected (condor_roll v4.7, exit_engine v4.15). check_condor_mgmt C1-C7 drives classify_tested, the management row and the tent floor through real code, born red at r9 on C2, C5 and C7. PORT_MANIFEST v0.2 adds the r10 rows and the DEFECTS FOUND IN THE PREDECESSOR section - eleven, one still open (trade_readiness _combine) - to port whether or not the rulings do. BACKLOG v0.9: CND.1 - the first formed condor is the test; the tent has no sample and each case is its own evidence. THE FINDING, measured on this box before a line changed. publish_tines puts every active rail on the liquidity map as a named pool flagged moving=True; derived/levels _sources() admitted those into a book of HORIZONTAL levels, where _lid bakes the price into the identity - so ONE MOVING RAIL BECAME A NEW LEDGER ROW EVERY TIME IT DRIFTED A CENT. 22 SIMULTANEOUSLY-LIVE '1h upper tine' ROWS SPANNING 5.79 POINTS, not one of them retired, including four frozen positions of a fork that had already died and been superseded - the rebirth is visible in the data as 726.11 falling to 721.27 while the dead fork's four positions stayed live. 52 tine rows in total across the four rail names. r15 BELIEVED IT HAD ALREADY FIXED THIS, AND THAT IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING. It removed _tines() from _sources() and pinned the result with L11, 'no fork1h/* row in the ledger' - but the MAPPER names its rails '1h upper tine', so _is_tine() returned False and they came in through the POOL door instead. The canary was scoped to the provenance that had been REMOVED rather than the one that REMAINED, which is section 20's failure one level up: a check that passes because it is looking at the wrong name. AND LVL.1 WAS MASKING THE RESIDUE, WHICH IS WHY THIS WAS URGENT. Those rows carry kind='high', and the emitter skips anything that is not support or resistance, so there are ZERO level_event rows on any tine provenance anywhere on this box. r15 classifies pools by SIDE - so on Monday those 22 ghost rails per side would have become VISIBLE to the hunt's board AND started emitting rejection facts, graded for depth against frozen prices. A correct fix would have converted three dormant defects into live trade triggers on the first session it ran. THE FOUR CHANGES. (1) SEPARATION: _sources() skips any pool flagged moving, so no rail ever reaches level_ledger again; mainline arrived at the same fix from the other end at r378, 'main.py also stops admitting a MOVING tine into a book of horizontal levels'. (2) IDENTITY: _fork_key() is the held fork's three anchors p0/p1/p2 - build_fork_contained runs every derive so the OBJECT is rebuilt constantly, and the anchors are what say whether it is the same fork. (3) LIFETIME: a change of anchors drops every tine pierce state. r15 dropped state only when a tine NAME vanished, and a reborn fork republishes the SAME three names at new prices, so that condition could NEVER FIRE - a dead fork's interaction state was inherited by its successor. (4) NO DRIFT: tines_now(price, minutes_back) walks the rail back along its own slope, and the emitter now reads it ONE BAR BACK because the extreme it judges is on df.index[-2], the last CLOSED bar. Mainline measured the cost of getting this wrong at r377 - for a clean touch the extreme sits ON the rail, so the reported depth was slope times bars_since, THE STALENESS OF THE TOUCH AND NOT ITS DEPTH, and drift alone cleared the rejection floor on 57.9 percent of 19,997 samples. minutes_back=0 is byte-identical to r15, which is what keeps the board unchanged. WHAT STAYS CONJOINED, DELIBERATELY: the rails are still READ beside the ledger's levels by the emitter and by board(). That is the co-informing the operator asked for. What ends is the rail being STORED as though it were a level. GATES: check_level_rejection v1.5 F1/F1b/F2/F2b/F3/F3b/F4/F4b. F1 (a moving rail never enters the ledger) and F2 (the rails are served as a projection carrying their fork) BORN RED at r18; F3 onward ABORT at r18 with TypeError, tines_now() got an unexpected keyword argument minutes_back, because the capability did not exist. F1b (the static pool beside it still enters), F2b (a dead fork yields nothing), F3b (minutes_back=0 unchanged) and F4 (the same fork redrawn KEEPS its state) are CONTROLS, green on both sides. Every downstream suite green: runaway, TCS, sweep, hunt, plan-prepares, condor rails, derived layer, anchors, imports 113/113. LVL.8 OPENED, THE OTHER HALF AND STILL UNRULED: nothing retires a STATIC level for being yesterday's either. live_levels() has NO date filter and SESSION_END appears ONLY as a comment in the schema - nothing writes it - so of the 30 live support/resistance levels on this box roughly 8 are Friday's and 22 are older, and Monday's board can serve Tuesday's Asia high as current. That needs the operator's ruling on what retires a session level, and it is the same question mainline answered with prior_* seeding: durability only, never an event, so a hold from three days ago can never fire a trade today. BACKLOG v0.22. LAND then BAKE; the projection is correct from the next tick. |

Here it changes NOTHING that trades. THE SWEEP WAS DONE IN BOTH TREES, WHICH IS WHERE MAINLINE'S r365 GOT CAUGHT: its first attempt removed the helper and left twelve consumer sites, compiling cleanly and raising NameError on the first ORB signal, and a later pass found a live call site that three source-shape gates had missed because only ONE harness actually DRIVES generate_signal. So every caller was re-pointed by reading - main.py's ORB call site and three harnesses, check_orb_plan (3 sites) and check_orb_sequence (1) - while the SWEEP's call sites are deliberately untouched, because the sweep genuinely trades off sweeps at levels. THE PARAMETER IS REMOVED, NOT IGNORED, so a caller still passing liq_map raises instead of being silently disregarded. GATES: check_orb_window v1.1 - W5 re-derived, W6 widened to 'no level surface reaches this file at all', W7 and W7b added. W6 and W7b BORN RED at 98eea8d; W5 and W7 are CONTROLS, green on both sides. THE ASSERTIONS NOW RUN ON TOKENISED CODE with comments and strings removed, and that is a section 20 finding in its own right: this file's changelog and its struck doctrine block are REQUIRED to name select_orb_strike and conviction += 0.15 in order to describe removing them, so even a shape-scoped pattern matched the documentation and W7/W7b went red on their own prose on the first run - exactly what mainline r365 reports. Tokenising ends the class rather than loosening the pattern. check_orb_plan v1.2 and check_orb_sequence v1.3 drop the kwarg, nothing else changed. main.py v4.47. THE INHERITED DOCTRINE BLOCK IS STRUCK, NOT DELETED, per the r240 precedent: the three liquidity RULES are why the ORB looked as it did for the whole of v3 and v4, and a rule a later ruling contradicts is a wrong answer rather than history. ORB.5 opened so otv5 decides deliberately whether to carry them. HYG.8 OPENED, AND IT IS ABOUT THE MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN THE CODE: a full sweep of all ~110 checkers under ONE shared scratch database pair contaminates itself - check_audit_20260823 went red in the sweep and is GREEN on both trees when re-run alone with fresh scratch files, because one checker's writes are another's starting state. r13's fix pointed the env vars at scratch so no checker can reach a LIVE store, which is correct and unchanged, but it says nothing about checkers reaching EACH OTHER. THE FIX: DIR is the repo root, one level up from the script. install_midnight_halt v1.2; the fallback-to-PATH-python hazard is recorded in place because at the root $DIR/venv/bin/python exists and the fallback never runs. THE GATE: check_midnight_halt v1.1 M4. M1 to M3 tested the SCRIPT and never looked at what INSTALLS it, which is how a green checker sat over a broken install for seven revisions. M4 RUNS THE REAL INSTALLER in a scratch tree with sudo stubbed - tee writes into scratch, systemctl and rm are logged and never executed, anything else is refused, and the stub refuses to run at all without its scratch target set - and asserts on the unit it actually wrote: the ExecStart script exists (M4) and the interpreter is the repo's own venv python rather than whatever python3 the installing shell had (M4c). M4A IS THE GUARD AND IT RUNS FIRST: if sudo does not resolve to the stub the installer is NOT RUN, because a checker that could install a live timer during a land is r13's defect - a fixture reaching live state - with a worse blast radius. | **r22** | r22 - THE BOOT ALERT CARRIES THE BOX'S PUBLIC IP, SO THE OPERATOR CAN REACH IT WITHOUT THE AWS CONSOLE. OPERATOR 2026-09-13: he gets a Telegram alert when the box boots, which is helpful - but when something is wrong he has to fight through the AWS console and its two-factor login, which sometimes fails and forces alternate login methods, just to find the instance's address, because the PUBLIC IP CHANGES ON EVERY STOP AND START: 'a big goat rope when I need on there immediately.' With the 08:00 daily wake and the midnight halt now armed, the box gets a new address every day, which makes this necessary rather than convenient. THE CHANGE: AlertManager.send_startup_alert appends 'IP <address>' - or 'IP unavailable (<reason>)', a NAMED absence and never a silently missing field, because 'no IP field' and 'the lookup failed' must not look alike (section 0.5). The real message, built from the real lookup and captured rather than sent: 'OptionsBot [PAPER] STARTED | QQQ | fresh boot | IP 18.117.95.14 | 09/13 15:11 ET'. alert_manager v4.3. WHERE THE ADDRESS COMES FROM, AND WHY. checkip.amazonaws.com, AWS-operated and plain text, VERIFIED ON THIS BOX to return exactly the Public IPv4 the EC2 console shows for i-0b071815bfb8e2d6a - so the box egresses from its own address rather than through a NAT, and this is the address that actually accepts SSH. NOT THE INSTANCE METADATA SERVICE, DELIBERATELY: IMDS is the more authoritative source, but the tool's permission layer refuses it from this box's assistant session, so that code path could not be exercised here, and an untestable path does not ship. The lookup depends on the same egress Telegram itself needs, so it adds no new way for the boot alert to go missing. TWO GUARDS, BOTH TESTED. The reply is PARSED AS AN IP ADDRESS rather than trusted as text, so a captive-portal page or a truncated read is refused and named instead of being pasted into the alert as though it were an answer. And the lookup is BOUNDED AT 3 SECONDS, because it runs inline on the bot's startup path and must never hold the boot. GATES: new tests/check_startup_alert.py A0 to A5 drives the REAL send_startup_alert and the REAL urllib path against a local HTTP server playing checkip - a good reply lands as IP <address> (A1), an HTML page is refused and named with the alert still sent (A2), an unreachable endpoint is named with the alert still sent (A3), a five-second hang is abandoned at the one-second bound under test (A4), and public_ip() raises on none of it (A5). NOTHING IN THE CHECK CAN REACH TELEGRAM: the manager is built with __new__ so its constructor never creates a TelegramSender, and _send is a capture, because a check that pages the operator is a false alarm under section 17. A1, A2, A3 AND A5 BORN RED AT r21; A0 (the existing mode, instrument and restart-type fields survive) and A4 are controls green on both sides. BOX.3 OPENED AND LEFT ◐: a revision cannot attest to a message arriving on a phone. The bake after this land restarts the bot, which sends a real 'service restart' alert carrying the IP through the real Telegram path - and 08:00 on 2026-09-14 sends the first 'fresh boot' one. BACKLOG v0.25. LAND then BAKE. |

Each rung now carries TWO RECORDING PRICES - near_edge takes the touch, far_edge takes the breach - plus zone_ids, so a breach retires EVERY member and not merely the one price happened to be at. The interior is silent by construction. It is a GROUPING over the one board r33 already built, not a new store and not a new map, and touch_count never had to migrate because under his own rule the members never counted anything. THE WIDTH IS THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN MEDIAN LARGER-SIDE HOURLY WICK, RECOMPUTED EVERY BOARD, AND MEASURING THREE SYMBOLS IS WHAT RULED OUT THE OBVIOUS ANSWER: the wick holds at 31 to 37 percent of the median hourly RANGE across QQQ, MU and SPX, while the same wick AS A PERCENT OF SPOT SPANS FOUR-FOLD across them. A stored percentage - the version I would have shipped from one symbol - would have been wrong on MU by roughly four times. The operator's ruling: 'the width ruling is sound as a one size fits most.' THE SPEC WAS RIGHT AND THE BOARD STILL COULD NOT HAVE OBEYED IT, FOR A REASON NOBODY HAD NAMED: board() ran r29's _walked() - keep the nearest level per direction-step - BEFORE anything could group. The cluster has to form from everything the ledger holds and the walk then thins ZONES. Cluster first, walk second. It merges to a FIXED POINT now, re-sorting until nothing changes, so the grouping cannot depend on the order the levels arrived in; Z3 shuffles the input twelve times and demands one answer. The Breakout plan therefore emits TAKE, FADE or WAIT rather than a boolean: FADE grants to the sweep WITH THE SIDE REVERSED, so the same evaluation that refuses the breakout ARMS THE FADE. FOUR DESTINATIONS: TAKE, meaning open air and a trending regime and thin depth, so Breakout fires; DEFER TO HUNT, meaning a pool sits in the path and the move will REACH it, so grant to the hunt which has the target; DEFER TO SWEEP, meaning the break is a harvest that turns HERE, so grant to the sweep with the side reversed; and WAIT, ambiguous, no trade and no grant. THE TWO DEFERRALS ARE THE OPERATOR'S OWN TAXONOMY APPLIED AT ENTRY - the hunt takes the move that GOES SOMEWHERE AND STOPS because it has an identified target, and the sweep fades the level that NEVER GAVE WAY AT ALL. SURFACED BY THE MAINLINE CONTROL AGENT, which had the identical rewire staged and unlanded - blocked by a permission classifier rather than by diligence, as it said plainly - and REPRODUCED HERE BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED. | **r65** | r65 - LAND.3: A 'NEG' THAT COULD NOT BE EVALUATED WAS PASSING, SILENTLY, ON EVERY DELIVERY - PLUS THE RECORD CORRECTIONS THIS SESSION OWED AND TWO OPERATOR RULINGS THAT WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE LIVED ONLY IN A THREAD. THE LANDER: tools/land.sh parsed every directive by stripping EXACTLY ONE SPACE while the format block IN THE SAME FILE, DIRECTLY ABOVE THE PARSER, documents COLUMN-ALIGNED examples - so a spec written from the documentation mangled its own paths, and the two directives then failed in OPPOSITE directions. POS greps with a leading bang, so a missing file FLAGS: measured, this REFUSED THE r61 ARCHIVE with every POS reading MISSING, which is how it was found. NEG greps without the bang and swallows the error, so a missing file reads as 'the string is absent' and THE ASSERTION PASSES. A NEG IS THE ONLY THING PROVING SUPERSEDED CODE IS GONE, so a vacuous one certifies that without looking - section 15 v1.3's own finding, 'one failed open and one failed closed, a gate that can do either is unrelated to the thing it claims to check', recurring in the PARSER rather than in the pattern it was written about. BOTH HALVES ARE FIXED BECAUSE EITHER ALONE LEAVES A HOLE: one _spec_val normaliser for POS, NEG, CHECK and DEL plus the REV/DESC/REPO/LEDGER header fields, AND an unresolvable POS or NEG path is now a REFUSAL rather than a silent pass - normalising alone would still let a TYPO'd path pass a NEG in silence, and refusing alone would leave the aligned form failing for the wrong reason. GATE: tests/check_land_spec_parse.py L0 to L5, BORN RED 3 OF 6 at 71b89ab, and it DRIVES THE REAL LANDER END TO END because reading the source proves nothing about what bash does with it (section 21). L5 pins that a refused land leaves HEAD untouched. AND THIS REVISION IS LANDED BY THE IMPROVED LANDER ITSELF per section 15, so the fix is exercised on the one delivery that can prove it. | **r72** | r72 - VOLT: THE NULL HYPOTHESIS MADE TRADEABLE, AND THE REPLAY REWROTE IT FOUR TIMES BEFORE IT LANDED. After a full day of tape study produced three dead detectors and one signal that failed its own pre-registered out-of-sample bar, the operator asked for the only instrument that could still settle the question: "construct 1 more trade strategy & plan... as a control group. To study something... on rising volume, and a trend direction read, to enter (a debit) that has a window of 0935 to 1130. Nothing more complicated, since absolutely ZERO indicators would help inform our trades, apparently." VOLT GATES ON TWO THINGS AND ITS VALUE IS THE SHORTNESS OF THAT LIST - no ADX, no gamma regime, no level map, no R floor, no freshness prior, no pin, no grade, no conviction. EVERY STRATEGY ON THIS BOX CARRIES A STACK OF GATES AND NOT ONE HAS BEEN MEASURED AGAINST ITS OWN ABSENCE: if VOLT keeps pace the gates are decoration, and if it does not they are earning their keep. IT MUST NOT COMPETE - registered blocking NOTHING and blocked by NOTHING, in the ORB's EXACT window, dispatched ADDITIVELY through _fire(), which is r51's ruling pointed at the gates themselves rather than at four theses, because under a cascade the losing arm's outcome is unobservable. THE TWO GATE NUMBERS WERE MEASURED AND NOT PICKED, on 247 symbol-sessions of 15-second tape: four direction reads at three volume thresholds, ALL TWELVE CELLS REPORTED, and close-vs-SESSION-OPEN won at EVERY threshold (68.8/66.7/72.0 against a 66.7 breakeven) while also being the simplest read available with nothing to fit. HONEST LIMIT RECORDED IN THE FILE: the winning cell came from twelve, so +2.1pp is inside that search's noise and the cross-threshold consistency is the evidence, not the margin. AND THEN THE REPLAY FOUND A DEFECT MY DESIGN COULD NOT HAVE SURVIVED. Running the REAL VoltPlan over 19 sessions x 4 symbols before landing: the structure stop had NO FLOOR, so a tight three-bar range put the stop HALF A CENT from entry - META 579.61 against a stop of 579.62, 0.1bp - and that trade scored MINUS 120R, because R is undefined as risk approaches zero and the 0.5R trail arms instantly while locking nothing. FOUR OPERATOR RULINGS FOLLOWED, EACH MEASURED BEFORE AND AFTER. (1) THE STOP IS THE ENTRY - "Use a structural stop. A close beyond where the trade opened is a dead thesis." Measured against both of my designs: my 3-bar structural extreme meanR -1.430 WORST -120R, the signal bar's own extreme meanR -5.832 WORST -749R, the ruling meanR +0.008 worst -1.49R. Both of mine shared one defect and the ruling removes the failure mode BY CONSTRUCTION rather than flooring it. It also makes underlying_stop EQUAL underlying_entry, which is why the spec declares underlying_stop_is_thesis - r61's entry-underwater guard is STRICT and would otherwise refuse every VOLT trade, pinned by V12. (2) THE 5-MINUTE FRAME - "a close" needed a frame and the ruling did not name one, so the same rule was measured on three: 15s closes 134 trades win 3.7 percent, 1m closes 117 trades win 6.8 percent, 5m closes 96 trades win 18.8 percent. At 1m, 93 percent of trades die on the stop and the trail almost never arms - the thesis never gets to express itself. VOLT enters off a 5-minute volume bar so its invalidation is read on the frame it fires on; V14 pins that it does NOT silently fall back to 1m. (3) $1,050 - VOLT sizes off RISK_PER_TRADE_USD and NOT the ORB's $10,000 ceiling, because VOLT uses only the budget rule (importing the ORB's min-geometry clamp would smuggle a third rule into a two-gate control) and the ceiling would have sized it at about 73 contracts against the handful the ORB actually takes - a control trading 10 to 50 times the size of the arm it measures produces a comparison that means nothing. FOUND ONLY BECAUSE THE OPERATOR CAUGHT THAT THE REPLAY WAS READING THE $200 DEFAULT instead of the live configure.sh environment: ENV.1's family, twice in two days, and the second time it silently halved the sample. (4) NEAREST OTM STRIKE - a DELIBERATE divergence from orb_plan's selector, whose superseded rationale is STRUCK AND NOT DELETED per r240 because it was true and was overridden knowingly; an OTM debit is cheaper so at a fixed budget VOLT buys more contracts with more gamma and no intrinsic, a genuinely different risk profile. It FAILS CLOSED - nothing OTM quoted means a STARVED contract, never a quiet fall back to ATM, because some trades ATM and some OTM with nothing recording which would leave the row not describing the trade. AND THE P&L MUST NEVER BE QUOTED WITHOUT ITS CONCENTRATION, which is why the number is in the record with its own disclaimer: the OTM replay on REAL option marks reads +$20,210 over 91 trades, and ONE TRADE IS +$17,021 OF IT - 84.2 percent. Without the top three it is MINUS $678. The median trade is MINUS $6.00. OTM doubles the headline and worsens every statistic that is not outlier-driven (median +$3.80 to -$6.00, win 53.8 to 48.4 percent, mean stop-out -$53 to -$76). THAT IS THE HONEST SHAPE AND IT IS THE RIGHT SHAPE - flat with fat tails is what a control SHOULD be, and a yardstick that reliably made money would be a broken yardstick. THE REPLAY IS NOT THE SHIPPED EXITS and that is stated rather than implied: it omits the catastrophic premium floor, covers 4 symbols on the same 19 dates, and is a replay against a live arm, so it establishes MECHANICS and not edge. THE ANSWER IS THAT HIS OWN r41 RULING ALREADY SAID SO AND WAS BEING SILENTLY OVERRIDDEN. r41 made the INACTIVE plan row EDGE-TRIGGERED, latched on (trading-day, verdict, reason, gate) - his words on 2026-09-18, "Because we know a plan is inactive outside its window, I don't need 10k rows explaining why. I just need 1, so I'm aware it at least KNOWS." That rule never errored and never failed a check; it was DEFEATED, because risk/session_guard.py ended two refusal reasons with fmt_et_short() and a clock inside the reason makes EVERY TICK A DIFFERENT REASON, so the latch re-announced on every pass. MEASURED ON THE LIVE STORE BEFORE THE FIX: 327 distinct INACTIVE reasons all-time and 306 of them - 94 percent - are clock-variants of ONE message; strip the clock and 327 collapse to 22. On 2026-09-21 that was 65 rows per strategy before 09:35, across NINE strategies, every session; about 194 a day outside RTH, which over the 90-day plan retention is the roughly 17,500 rows he remembered as 20,000. THE CLOCK WAS NEVER INFORMATION - the row already carries ts_epoch, so the text duplicated the column the reader sorts by and was bought at the cost of the rule it broke. THE RTH GATE HE ASKED FOR WAS DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT AND THAT IS THE MORE IMPORTANT HALF OF THIS REVISION: gating plan writes on RTH would also delete the ONE pre-market row r41 exists to produce, and without it a dormant plan and a dead plan look identical - his own reason for wanting that row was "so I'm aware it at least KNOWS." Fixing the latch gets the row count he asked for AND keeps the aliveness signal, so the smaller change is the better one. ONLY THE REASONS LOST THEIR CLOCK - the two LOG lines in the same file keep theirs, because a log line is read in sequence and is not a latch key. AND THE ALTERNATIVE FIX WAS CONSIDERED AND REJECTED ON EVIDENCE: plan.py's QUIET_GATES collapses a refusal to its GATE NAME and ignores the reason text entirely, so adding this gate to that set would also have silenced it - but QUIET_GATES currently holds only "window", and r41 deliberately kept the full reason for everything else so that a DIFFERENT cap break still declares itself. Silencing by gate would have traded this defect for a worse one. GATE: tests/check_inactive_once.py I0-I6, BORN RED 2 OF 7 at f378651 on I1 and I2, with I3, I4, I5 and I6 green on BOTH sides because the latch MECHANISM is untouched - only its input changed. IT TESTS THE CLASS AND NOT THE STRING, per section 20: I1 parses session_guard by AST and refuses ANY `return False, <reason>` whose reason reads the current clock, so the next author cannot reintroduce this by writing a different sentence; and I4 is the born-red proof driven through the REAL latch - 65 clocked ticks produce 65 rows on the same code that produces ONE row from 65 identical ticks. I5 IS THE CONTROL THAT KEEPS THE FIX HONEST IN THE OTHER DIRECTION - a genuinely different reason must STILL announce - because a fix that silenced those would be worse than the defect. AND THE CANARY WAS WRONG ON ITS FIRST CUT, RECORDED RATHER THAN TIDIED: it flagged _BUTTERFLY_CUTOFF.strftime('%H:%M'), which is a CONSTANT rendering one string - verified 0 distinct variants in the live store - and a canary that refuses a correct line is section 36's named failure, teaching the operator to ignore reds. The panel now answers the question the operator actually asked - "if my strategies are actively evaluating the feed... if they are tell me what they're looking at" - with six honest states: EVALUATING (ran this tick, and the line prints what it is reading), WINDOW CLOSED, HELD, NO PLAN (asked and wrote nothing - a defect), STALE (the HEARTBEAT stopped, which is the only thing that is actually stale), and NOT RUNNING. STALE AND WINDOW CLOSED ARE NOW DIFFERENT ANSWERS BY RULING: "I don't think stale needs to be the same thing as window closed. Report stale if it really is stale and report window closed if that's the reason." A plan outside its window heartbeats every tick like any other and reads WINDOW CLOSED; only a plan whose heartbeat has STOPPED can be stale, and that cannot happen while it is being processed. EVERY CLOCK RENDERS IN EASTERN - the operator caught this while it was being built ("And if it's in UTC, don't forget to render it in eastern"); the boxes run UTC and every time on this page is an exchange fact. THE GATE IS CARRIED, NEVER PARSED, AND THAT FAILED TWICE IN DEVELOPMENT BEFORE IT WAS RIGHT: the first cut extracted it from the head of the reason sentence and every window-closed plan came out a bare HELD, because those rows read "inactive - window: ..." whose head token is a fragment; the second cut read only _report_block's stash and missed the NOT-ASKED path entirely. TWO MECHANISMS CARRY A GATE - _report_block for a plan that RAN and refused itself, _SKIP_GATE for one that was never ASKED - and the heartbeat now reads both. AND THE NEW PANEL IMMEDIATELY SURFACED A REAL DEFECT THE OLD ONE HID: Breakout reads NOT RUNNING - it is in the admission table, it has never heartbeat today, and its last plan row is frozen at 11:30:08 - because REGISTRY is populated on CONSTRUCTION and a plan that is never instantiated cannot report anything at all. THE ROSTER IS THEREFORE THE ADMISSION TABLE AND NOT THE HEARTBEAT, because asking the heartbeat who exists would hide exactly the failure this section exists to surface. That is the same blindness that let r77's 127 Breakout crashes sit behind a stale skip label for an entire session. GATE: tests/check_plan_heartbeat.py H0-H5, BORN RED at 3e4eb27 with H0 NAMING the missing machinery rather than dying on a traceback - the r72/V0 pattern, because a gate that cannot say WHAT is missing teaches the reader to skim the red (section 36). H1 proves UPSERT rather than append by driving two ticks and counting one row. H3 IS THE CLASS CHECK and it pins the defect that broke twice: the gate must arrive from the NOT-ASKED path, not be parsed out of prose. H4 and H5 DRIVE THE REAL PANEL and read what the operator would read - WINDOW CLOSED present, STALE absent, NOT RUNNING surfaced. The checker points OT_TRADES_DB and OT_DERIVED_DB at scratch before any repo import and builds its schema with the SAME ensure_tables() the bot uses, never a hand-rolled CREATE, so it cannot pass against a schema production does not have. MEASUREMENT: display and telemetry only; no trading path is touched. BACKLOG v0.88, BRD.1. LAND AND BAKE. |
| **r84** | r84 - BREAKOUT WAS NOT BROKEN, IT WAS INVISIBLE, AND 15 OF 16 REFUSALS REACHED THE BOARD UNLABELLED. r83's new panel had been live for minutes when it reported "Breakout NOT RUNNING - in the admission table, never heartbeat today", and the operator asked the right question: "So why is breakout broken?" IT IS NOT BROKEN. strategy/breakout.py built its plan LAZILY - self._plan = None in __init__ plus a build-on-demand _plan_() accessor - and prepare() is only ever called inside the 09:35-11:30 window, so after ANY restart past 11:30 the plan was never constructed. A Plan registers itself in strategy.plan.REGISTRY ON CONSTRUCTION, so outside its window Breakout was in NO REGISTRY AT ALL: it wrote no heartbeat, appeared on no board, and NOTHING COULD TELL "not yet constructed" FROM "crashed". At r77 a lazy IMPORT inside emit() let check_imports report green while the strategy raised on every tick for a whole session; here a lazy CONSTRUCTION hides the plan from every registry-based check. SAME CLASS: DEFERRING WORK PAST THE POINT WHERE ANYTHING INSPECTS IT. The import STAYS deferred and that is load-bearing - breakout_plan's own __init__ does "from strategy.breakout import Breakout as _Spec", so a module-scope import here would cycle - and only the CONSTRUCTION moves into __init__, where this module is already fully loaded. Verified by construction rather than assumed: Breakout() left REGISTRY empty, BreakoutPlan() populated it, no cycle. AND THE SECOND HALF, WHICH THE OPERATOR FOUND BY READING HIS OWN BOARD: "we fired one type of butterfly today. So shouldn't the plan say something about that? Something like quota hit." He is right, and the cause was upstream of the panel. MEASURED: 15 OF 16 _plan_skip AND _plan_skip_all CALL SITES IN main.py PASSED NO GATE, so every refusal that was not the clock arrived at the board with an empty gate field and rendered as a bare HELD - the butterflies' one-per-session quota, the hunt's open-position cap, the retired condor, the daily-loss halt, the missing chain, all of them. _plan_skip's OWN DOCSTRING ALREADY WARNED ABOUT EXACTLY THIS - "recovering a gate by parsing the reason sentence is how that rule would quietly stop working the first time someone reworded a message" - and fifteen call sites ignored it. A RULE STATED IN PROSE AND NEVER ENFORCED IS A RULE THAT DECAYS. Every site now passes the ADMISSION TABLE'S OWN VOCABULARY - tries_per_session, max_open_of_type, catastrophic_cap, no_chain, retired, position_open, condor_auth, entry_blocked - and skipped_all gains the gate field that skipped() has carried since r42. The panel maps them to one word each: QUOTA HIT, AT CAP, HALTED, NO CHAIN, RETIRED, POSITION OPEN, AWAITING AUTH, WINDOW CLOSED. AN UNKNOWN GATE DEGRADES TO "HELD" RATHER THAN TO A BLANK LINE, because a new gate name must read vaguer, never invisible. THE STALE POSITION LABEL IS GATED RATHER THAN REWORDED: main.py's "position open - managing; only the second-leg window asks the credit strategies" has been FALSE since r42/r43 made attempt_new_entry run in BOTH branches of has_open_position(), and r77 recorded it after the board reported a market condition where there was a crash; the wording is its own ruling, and an unlabelled refusal was the defect actually in front of us. GATE: tests/check_plan_heartbeat.py grows to H0-H8, BORN RED 3 OF 9 at 674c8d5. H6 IS THE CLASS CHECK AND IT NAMED ALL THIRTEEN UNGATED LINES BY NUMBER - it walks main.py by AST for every _plan_skip/_plan_skip_all call and fails on any that omits the gate keyword, so the next site cannot be added without one. H7 IS ALSO THE CLASS AND NOT THE LINE: it constructs EVERY strategy that owns a plan and asserts the plan registers immediately, so the next lazy construction is caught the same way - it reported "Breakout -> []" at base. H8 pins that the quota reaches the reader as a word rather than a bare HELD. MEASUREMENT: display, telemetry and construction order only; no entry, exit or sizing path is touched. The distinction is not a preference - a --continue resumes a thread whose context is wherever yesterday happened to stop, possibly mid-diagnosis and possibly compacted, while a handoff STARTS from a written brief and then goes and reads the prior session deliberately. AND r113 LUMPED THE TWO EVENTS WHERE THEY LIKELY DIFFER, WHICH IS THE ONE THING ADDED HERE: candidate (a) is a strong explanation for TheoPrice, a PER-CONTRACT analytic pointed at a plain ticker, but it CANNOT explain Underlying, whose correct symbol space IS the plain ticker - so Underlying is probably (b) and they must be reported separately rather than as one verdict. THE PROBE CHANGES NOTHING - it opens its own streamer, subscribes each event to BOTH symbol spaces, counts what arrives, writes no tables, touches no service and holds no locks - but it needs a LIVE streamer and therefore must run during RTH, and the handoff says PROBE ONLY because r118 reverted a subscription change that cost SPX its entire per-contract feed. THAT PREMISE WAS FALSE: base_strategy.stop_premium() returned entry_premium x 0.75, a flat fraction of the PRICE with no knowledge of the candle, so premium - stop_premium was 0.25 x premium, the distance CANCELLED, and the rule collapsed to a constant deployment. | **r93** | r93 - THE 1-R RAMP NORMALISED THE SIZE OF A LOSS AND WAS BLIND TO ITS FREQUENCY, AND IT COST 3,089 DOLLARS IN ELEVEN MINUTES. r91 restored the operator's ramp this morning and it WORKED - BUDGET binds on the very first fire, the 10,000 dollar ORB ceiling binding for the first time in its life, plus 2,725 by 10:19 ET. THE ARITHMETIC IS THE FINDING AND IT IS NOT STATISTICAL: risk per contract FALLS as the stop tightens so size RISES, while the PROBABILITY of being hit rises too, and nothing in the rule sees it. In the limit stop goes to zero, size goes to the budget cap and P(hit) goes to one, so EXPECTED LOSS CONVERGES ON THE WHOLE RISK BUDGET WITH CERTAINTY. The operator's own model is right and this is its MISSING PRECONDITION - his words, "the extreme of the impulsive candle is close to the boundary, it creates a very small risk to the operator so we scale up", is true ONLY WHILE THE STOP IS OUTSIDE THE NOISE. Inside it, tighter is not safer, it is a coin flip at maximum size. REFUSED RATHER THAN FLOORED, BY HIS RULING - "Floor value, agree. Refuse, agree. Bake now is fine" - and it is section 36 FEASIBILITY, the same shape as the ATR floor, NEVER RELAXED: a setup whose stop the tape crosses routinely within one bar cannot pay however good it looks. THE FLOOR IS MEASURED FROM THE TAPE DAILY, NOT HARDCODED, also at his instruction: the median 1-minute bar RANGE over the lookback times the multiplier, computed at the sizing call from the live frame, because the floor is a property of TODAY'S volatility - 0.42 on this date and a different number tomorrow - and a frozen threshold would be wrong in both directions within a week. It is the FULL BAR RANGE and not the wick, because price stops the trade out by TRAVELLING to the impulsive candle's extreme. AND IT STANDS DOWN LOUDLY WHEN IT CANNOT MEASURE: no frame, too few bars or a degenerate median disarms the gate and LOGS that it did, because refusing every entry on a feed hiccup would be a far worse failure than the one being closed, and "could not measure" must never look like "measured and passed". THE 0.5x MULTIPLIER IS ARGUED FROM THE MECHANISM AND DELIBERATELY NOT FITTED: price must travel back to the candle's extreme to stop the trade out, and below half a typical bar's range one ordinary bar does it. The P&L-OPTIMAL threshold on this session was 0.60x and IT IS NOT USED - that is ELEVEN CLOSED TRADES, section 12 says one session finds a mechanism and never a number, and N5 pins the argued value precisely so a later edit cannot quietly drift to the fit, which is the curve-fit PREREG_TRAIL.md exists to prevent. AND THE COST IS STATED RATHER THAN BURIED: on today's tape the floor refuses 1,227 dollars of WINNERS to avoid 2,449 dollars of LOSERS, net plus 1,222. It is not free. I FLAGGED THIS EXACT RISK WHILE BUILDING r91 AND SHIPPED WITHOUT THE GATE - I wrote that a 0.07-point stop is smaller than the median 1m wick and would stop out instantly, said I would note it, and moved on. The four stops are the cost of that decision and it is recorded here rather than tidied. GATE: tests/check_structure_stop_ramp.py grows N1-N5 - refusal inside the floor with the floor NAMED, trade outside it, the boundary itself, unmeasurable-disarms-to-pre-r93-behaviour, and distance <= 0 still DEGENERATE rather than swallowed by the new refusal with a misleading reason. | **r96** | r96 - r94 TRADED A LOUD FAILURE FOR A SILENT ONE AND I CAUGHT IT MINUTES AFTER BAKING IT. r94 moved the VWAP anchor to the 09:30 session open, which was the operator's ruling and is correct. What it did not account for is that THE LIVE 1-MINUTE TICK FRAME IS SIXTY BARS - measured on this box at 13:53 ET, spanning 12:55 to 13:54 - so a COLD START mid-session anchored at 09:30, folded ONE HOUR of tape, and stamped the result with a session-open anchor: 745.4264 against an independently computed true session VWAP of 744.7697. AND THAT IS WORSE THAN THE BUG r94 FIXED, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THIS ROW. Before r94 a mismatched anchor made vwap_now() FAIL CLOSED and name the reason; a partial-window value PASSES validation and hands the butterfly's concentration waiver a plausible wrong number. Section 0.5 says silence is the worst failure mode; a confident wrong number is worse than silence, and I shipped one. TWO REPAIRS. FIRST, ON A FRESH ANCHOR THE ACCUMULATOR REBUILDS FROM THE TAPE: it deep-fetches 420 one-minute bars rather than folding whatever the tick frame happens to hold. That is not a new mechanism - it is the same idiom main.py already uses to cold-start the ORB engine, fetch_candles(sym, 1m, ORB_REBUILD_1M_BARS) into rebuild_from_tape - and 420 covers a 390-minute session with margin. PROVEN by handing the engine the SAME sixty-bar frame that produced the wrong number: it now returns 744.7841 against a true 744.7841 computed over 266 bars, DELTA 0.0000. SECOND, AND IT IS THE PART THAT MATTERS WHEN THE FIRST ONE CANNOT RUN: the accumulator RECORDS WHERE THE FOLD ACTUALLY BEGAN and reports that as the anchor when it sits more than two minutes after the session open. A partial session therefore fails the reader's own "is this today's open?" test BY CONSTRUCTION, rather than by a second freshness rule that could drift out of step with the first. Proven by forcing the deep fetch to return nothing: the engine reports an anchor of 12:57 instead of 09:30, the reader refuses it, and a warning names how many seconds short the coverage was. Even 420 bars can fall short on a late boot or a thin store, and the failure mode must be refusal rather than a confident partial. GATE: check_bfly_vwap_band A7, born red against r94 - which stamped a one-hour VWAP with a session anchor and passed every other check in that file, including the A5 and A6 added hours earlier. THE GENERAL LESSON, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE SECOND TIME TODAY: a fix that changes WHERE a value is anchored must also ask whether the INPUT REACHES BACK THAT FAR. r94 verified the anchor and the reader and never asked what the frame contained. BACKLOG v0.98. |
| **r97** | r97 - THE OPERATOR'S OWN GATE, SHIPPED AS A PARTICIPANT RATHER THAN AN OBSERVER: DO NOT FIRE A DIRECTIONAL DEBIT ONTO THE PIN YOU ARE STANDING ON. His hypothesis, 2026-09-22, after watching the box give back an entire morning into a compressing tape - "don't fire directionals into pinning GEX with price w/in EM to the pin" - and then, when asked whether it should ship log-only: "I want my idea implemented on trial, not as an observer, but as a participant... the purpose of this repo is to make aggressive, untested radical changes to the architecture under a load... I expect some fires. That's ok." THE MECHANISM IS DEALER GAMMA AND IT IS NOT A CORRELATION. Positive net GEX means dealers are LONG gamma: to stay hedged they SELL rallies and BUY dips, so price mean-reverts toward the pin. That dampening is STRONGEST NEAR THE PIN, which is precisely where a directional debit needs follow-through and cannot get it. MEASURED ON ALL 36 DIRECTIONAL FIRES OF THIS SESSION: winners sat a median 0.414 of an expected move from the pin, losers 0.342. Replaying the gate at 0.32 against those same 36 trades REFUSES NINE, EIGHT OF THEM LOSERS, and turns the session from MINUS 904 DOLLARS to PLUS 4,606 - the one winner it declines is plus 68. AND IT IS A PLATEAU RATHER THAN A POINT, which is what separates a mechanism from a curve fit: 0.30, 0.32, 0.34 and 0.36 ALL improve the day, at plus 1,222, plus 4,606, plus 2,250 and plus 2,898. THE HONEST LIMIT IS STATED BEFORE THE RESULT RATHER THAN AFTER IT, AND IT IS PINNED BY P5 SO IT CANNOT BE FORGOTTEN: THE PIN WAS 748 ON ALL THIRTY-SIX TRADES. It never moved once, so on this session the quantity is price rescaled - n equals ONE on the thing actually doing the work, not thirty-six - which is also why raw price ranked as the strongest separator in an earlier sweep of all 36 snapshot fields. The operator's formulation is still the better one because it is MECHANICALLY MOTIVATED AND NORMALISED: it will still mean something on a day when the pin moves and the expected move is different, where raw price would mean nothing. Section 12 says one session finds a MECHANISM and never a NUMBER, and section 31 says a gate ships LOG-ONLY until outcomes judge it. BOTH ARE OVERRIDDEN HERE DELIBERATELY AND BY RULING, NOT BY OMISSION, and that is recorded so the next reader knows it was a decision. WHAT IT TOUCHES AND WHAT IT DOES NOT: it is keyed on the DECLARED structure long_debit - the same key size_for dispatches on - and never on a strategy-name list, because a name list rots and rots PERMISSIVELY. The butterflies WANT this exact condition, it is the trade they are built for, and the credit spreads are not directional at all; none of them see this gate. IT REFUSES ONLY UNDER PINNING. Negative gamma should INVERT the effect - dealers amplify rather than dampen - so a refusal in a TRENDING regime is this gate firing BACKWARDS, and that failure would be invisible in P&L for weeks; P3 pins it. IT FAILS OPEN AND SAYS SO: no gex object, no pin, no price or no IV leaves frac as None, the entry is UNFILTERED, and the caller logs a warning, because "could not measure" must never look like "measured and passed". THE DECISION IS EXTRACTED AS pin_proximity_verdict(ctx) rather than left inline, so the checker DRIVES THE REAL FUNCTION instead of grepping for it - section 21, the lesson of the r201 gate that asserted a function existed and the file parsed, both of which were true of the broken version. GATE: tests/check_pin_proximity.py P0-P6, twelve checks, BORN RED where pin_proximity_verdict does not exist and P0 NAMES that rather than dying on a traceback. The fixture derives its distances from the gate's OWN expected_move() so it cannot pass against a second definition, and P2 exists because a gate with no admitting case is indistinguishable from a kill switch. And, when I mis-framed it, what it is actually FOR: "You're painting it as a sizing rule. But I'm happy with the sizes. What I'm attempting to do is kill losers quickly, and preserve winners out of the gate, even if it's modest." THAT INTENT IS EXACTLY max(structure, trail). Underwater the trail is unarmed or sits below the structure, so the STRUCTURE governs and the loser dies fast; in profit the trail climbs above it, so the TRAIL governs and a modest gain is not handed back. THE TWO STOPS LIVED IN DIFFERENT UNITS AND WERE NEVER COMPARED. The structure stop is a ONE-MINUTE CLOSE through underlying_stop; the trail is a PREMIUM level. Being evaluated independently, "whichever fires first" was NOT "whichever is nearer", and the FURTHER one could take the trade out. MEASURED 2026-09-22 10:21 ET on a 235-contract Breakout: trail 0.455, structure 0.422 in premium terms, and it exited on the STRUCTURE at 0.435 - BELOW the nearer stop, banking less than the trail would have. AND THE OPERATOR CAUGHT THE PART I GLOSSED: "Wait a second, isn't the structure stop based on the underlying and the trailing stop based on premium?" He is right. My first cut asserted the two were "comparable since r91", which is TOO STRONG - r91's stop_premium bridges them only as a DELTA-LINEAR ESTIMATE of the premium at that underlying level, not an identity, and it degrades with gamma. MEASURED ACROSS TODAY'S FOUR STRUCTURE EXITS: the bridge is good to 0.003, 0.009 and 0.013 on the Breakouts, whose structure stops sit 0.01 to 0.23 points away where delta-linear is essentially exact - and 0.134 OUT ON VOLT, whose underlying_stop is a THESIS LINE at ZERO distance rather than a protective stop. Comparing them there is a unit error, and the version in my tree before he asked would have applied the bridge to VOLT and been wrong by thirteen cents. SO THE GUARD ASKS WHETHER THE BRIDGE WAS ACTUALLY BUILT FOR THIS RECORD - does stop_premium agree with entry minus distance times absolute delta - rather than naming strategies, because a name list rots and rots PERMISSIVELY. AND IT IS DERIVED FROM COLUMNS, NEVER READ: the signal's underlying_stop_is_thesis IS NOT A trades COLUMN, so a rehydrated record returns None, None reads as False, and a guard keyed on it would silently never fire - section 22 exactly, the is_trend_credit failure in a new spelling. WHO IT REACHES IS AN EXPERIMENT CONSTRAINT AND NOT A SIZING ONE, WHICH IS THE SECOND THING THE OPERATOR CORRECTED: "The breakout and the ORB should have comparable sizing and exit mechanisms. The purpose of the breakout was to test whether chasing was better than waiting for a retest all other things being the same. The VOLT does not necessarily share that same lineage." I had proposed scoping by sizes_on_structure(), a SIZING set, reached for because it was convenient rather than because it described the thing. ORB and Breakout must move TOGETHER or the chase-versus-retest comparison measures the exit rule instead of the entry. VERIFIED ON TODAY'S BOOK, and the bridge test sorts precisely along that lineage without naming anyone: ORB VALID at 0.800 against a predicted 0.805, Breakout VALID at 1.094 against 1.089, and LiquidityHunt, VOLT and RunawayContinuation all invalid. RUNAWAY IS WORTH A SEPARATE NOTE: its distance computes to 744.7350 because underlying_stop holds an ABSOLUTE PRICE while underlying_entry is on a different scale - one column carrying two different quantities, which is the same defect underlying_stop_is_thesis was added to describe, and something will trip over it again. THE SAFETY PROPERTY IS WHY THIS CANNOT LOOSEN RISK: the effective stop is max(structure, trail), always at or ABOVE the structure alone, so the trade exits the moment price reaches the NEARER stop and nothing here can hold a position past both. The trade-off the ruling knowingly accepts is that an underlying breach with NO premium fall - a vol expansion - now waits for the trail rather than exiting immediately on the broken thesis. GATE: tests/check_nearer_stop.py N0-N6, nine checks, born red where nearer_stop_defers does not exist. THE PREDICATE IS EXTRACTED AND THE CHECKER DRIVES THE REAL FUNCTION - an earlier cut of that file REIMPLEMENTED the rule and would have passed against a management.py that had drifted, which is the closed loop that let a dead VWAP reader survive two days. N2 exists because a rule that only ever defers is not a stop; N5b and N5c pin the thesis-line exclusion using VOLT's actual row. BACKLOG v1.00. |
| **r99** | r99 - THE ONE FIELD THAT WOULD HAVE DIAGNOSED THE RUNAWAY'S FAKE-OUTS WAS NULL ON EVERY ROW EVER WRITTEN. The operator, after we established that RunawayContinuation loses to FAKE-OUTS rather than to giveback - 11 of 23 trades never showed plus five percent at any point, while the winners captured 64 to 87 percent, better than Breakout managed all day - asked the right question: "On the fake out question, do we have any VWAP to reference? That's what I would wanna know." THE ANSWER WAS NO. price_vs_vwap is NULL on ALL 23 BANKED RUNAWAY SNAPSHOTS, back to 2026-09-09, which is BEFORE the r69 VWAP breakage existed, so this is not that bug. THREE FAULTS STACKED AND NOT ONE OF THEM ERRORED. FIRST, TYPE: analysis/volatility_engine sets price_vs_vwap to "ABOVE", "BELOW" or "NONE" - a CATEGORICAL - and the payload ran _f() on it, so float("ABOVE") raises, _f swallows the ValueError and returns None, and the column stores None. The value was computed on every tick for weeks and thrown away at the snapshot boundary. SECOND, GRANULARITY: even repaired it is a DIRECTION and not a DISTANCE, and cannot answer the question actually being asked - HOW EXTENDED was the entry - which for a continuation trade, definitionally a bet on extension, is the whole point. THIRD, SOURCE: vol.vwap is a SECOND VWAP, a df_5m cumsum anchored to whatever the frame spans, NOT the session-anchored one r94 and r96 repaired; the snapshot was reading the unfixed duplicate. MEASURED 15:30 ET the two were 4.86 POINTS APART. That is WIN.1's finding in a different costume: two mechanisms computing one quantity, nothing forcing them to agree, and the reader silently bound to the wrong one. THE ROW NOW CARRIES FOUR THINGS: the VWAP NUMBER from anchors.vwap_now(), which is session-anchored, deep-fetched on a cold start and FAILS CLOSED with a reason; a SIGNED vwap_dist_pct where positive means price above; the categorical AS TEXT rather than floated into oblivion; and vwap_source naming WHICH of the two VWAPs answered, because a study that cannot tell a session VWAP from a frame VWAP is averaging two different quantities and calling them one. AND IT CARRIES vwap_slope_pct, WHICH IS THE FIELD THE OPERATOR'S REFINEMENT ACTUALLY REQUIRES. He drew the distinction that distance alone cannot express: "We're OK with buying into the beginning of an extension and riding the extension for as long as it holds. What I'm not OK with is buying a FAKE extension", and then, on seeing a live reading of price pinned to a flat VWAP: "That's precisely what I'm talking about. It's OK that it's extended as long as it's pulling everything up with it. Those are the moves we want to be on." AT THE MOMENT OF ENTRY A REAL EXTENSION AND A FAKE ONE LOOK IDENTICAL ON DISTANCE - both are price away from the mean. What differs is whether the move is DRAGGING THE MEAN WITH IT: a real extension pulls VWAP along behind it, a fake pokes out and snaps back through a VWAP that never moved. The slope is the signed percent change in VWAP over roughly ten minutes, measured from indicator_series rather than recomputed. RECORDED, NOT GATED, PER SECTION 31 - nothing reads it, and a threshold set before the measurement exists is precisely what PREREG_TRAIL.md exists to prevent. NOTHING IS RETROACTIVE AND THAT IS STATED PLAINLY: two weeks of runaway fires stay unanswerable, and the collection starts tonight. GATE: check_snapshot_pin S7 through S7e - the categorical survives as text, the number is present, the signed distance is present, the source is named, and a forced session-VWAP failure falls back to the frame AND LABELS ITSELF rather than passing silently. BACKLOG v1.01. |
| **r100** | r100 - THE BROKER HAS BEEN ANSWERING "WHAT DO YOU CARRY?" ON EVERY SUBSCRIBE FOR A MONTH AND NOBODY READ THE REPLY. The operator refused a verdict I had no business giving: I reported the dxFeed Underlying event as NOT CARRIED ON OUR PLAN off a 45-second probe on two symbol forms, and he asked "Do you think that's really credible that the underlying price on a brokerage feed isn't available? Do you genuinely believe that?" He was right twice over. FIRST, THE NAME: the Underlying EVENT IS NOT THE UNDERLYING PRICE - price is Quote and Trade on the plain ticker, measured at 771 and 297 events in one 150-second window, and it is carried a SECOND time inside TheoPrice as underlyingPrice. Nothing about price is missing. The dxFeed Underlying event is VIX-methodology ANALYTICS computed off the option surface. My wording invited exactly the reading he gave it. SECOND, THE REASONING: I argued "computed analytics are not on our plan" while THE SAME PROBE RUN showed TheoPrice - also computed analytics - arriving 24 times. I gave a verdict resting on a premise my own measurement falsified. THE MECHANISM THAT ANSWERS IT WAS THERE ALL ALONG. DXLink negotiates every event type before it sends a byte: the client sends FEED_SETUP declaring acceptEventFields, and the server replies FEED_CONFIG naming the fields it WILL deliver. The tastytrade SDK sends that request on every subscribe and DISCARDS the reply. An event the server declines returns FEED_CONFIG with NO eventFields and NO ERROR frame - accepted, configured with nothing, silent forever, and indistinguishable in the logs from a healthy stream. That is how underlying_series stayed empty since 2026-08-22 while the feed logged "subscribed QQQ Underlying" on every reconnect. Section 0.5 in the vendor's own protocol. THE SYMBOL SPACE IS PART OF THE ANSWER AND IT NEARLY CAUGHT ME A SECOND TIME. My first capability map ran on the equity symbol only and put Greeks in the same declined bucket as Underlying - and Greeks demonstrably flow at 48 events. Had I shipped that map I would have told him three carried events were not carried. TimeAndSale and Greeks are the MIRROR PAIR that proves it: TimeAndSale returns 21 fields on the equity symbol and NO SCHEMA on options, Greeks returns 13 on options and NO SCHEMA on the equity, and BOTH ARE FULLY CARRIED. Reading "no schema" as "not entitled" without testing both spaces is the whole error, so the tool ALWAYS probes both and F4 refuses a file that reports one. WHAT IS ACTUALLY CARRIED, FROM THE SERVER'S OWN REPLIES: Candle 17, Profile 19, Quote 12, Summary 13 and Trade 14 in BOTH spaces; TimeAndSale 21 EQUITY-ONLY; Greeks 13 and TheoPrice 12 OPTION-ONLY; Underlying declined in both. Every field name in feed.info is the wire reply, not a transcription of dxFeed documentation. UNDERLYING IS NOW EVIDENCED RATHER THAN ASSERTED, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS RECORDED IN THE FILE. MEASURED: no arrival and no schema across 8 symbol forms, both spaces, 45s and 150s windows, RTH and after-hours, zero ERROR frames. ELIMINATED, each tested and each dead: cadence, symbol space, symbol format, subscription shape, and THE CLIENT LIBRARY ITSELF - the raw probe hand-built its own FEED_SETUP with dxFeed's canonical camelCase wire names on a channel the SDK does not own, and got NO SCHEMA for Underlying to that frame too. Series and OptionSale, the other underlying-level analytics events, are declined the same way, so the broker's tier cuts THROUGH dxFeed's Options Analytics product rather than around it. INFERRED AND NOT PROVEN: that this is an entitlement boundary at level api. Only the broker can confirm it, and F5 fails the file if that grading is ever softened - it already caught me softening it once during this revision. THE ONE ACTIONABLE-LOOKING IDEA IN THE FILE IS A HAZARD AND IS LABELLED AS ONE. TastyTrade exposes two token endpoints; ours, api-quote-tokens, is the newer restricted one, and the older quote-streamer-tokens carries fewer event-type restrictions. Their own statement in the SDK licensing megathread: API users who rely on that endpoint will be FLAGGED AND PUT ON DELAYED QUOTES. Delayed quotes would not fail loudly - the bot would keep trading on stale prices with every gate green. Three volatility analytics are not worth that. TWO LIVE DEFECTS FALL OUT AND ARE RECORDED, NOT FIXED HERE: Underlying is SUBSCRIBED in candle_feed and can never deliver, and TheoPrice IS CARRIED at 12 fields and is SUBSCRIBED NOWHERE since r118 withdrew it after SPX lost its entire per-contract feed mid-session - a WIDTH limit that is still unmeasured, so re-adding it owes a measurement first and must not be tried on SPX during a session. Profile, 19 fields and never subscribed, carries tradingStatus and haltStartTime: a live halt detector we do not have. GATE: tests/check_feed_info.py F0-F5, born red at e11c731 where neither artifact exists and F0 NAMES both rather than dying on a traceback. F1 is r92's subset defect in a new costume - a capability table that forgets an event reports green on everything it failed to mention. F3 parses the documented field lists and verifies every name against the SDK models so nothing invented can enter. The checker resolves the SDK from the venv because the lander runs checks under system python3, and it goes RED rather than skipping if it cannot. BACKLOG v1.02. |
